# Seed 70 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - the first run with the JIT at 32 bits and the four JIT genes: the genes on no front design (Iteration 498)

`seed 70 11h then experiment footprint.py --fastest --sample 16 --sizes 100,10000 --rounds 3 then experiment
review-checks.py spread then experiment appbench.py --zip ~/Downloads/appbench.zip --sample 16` at b4c0b9c (Iteration
487-488). 39,777 designs, generations 0-155: 31,545 alive, 8,232 screened; islands off. The front 161 designs (116 by
mutation, 27 borrowed, 18 by crossover); the JIT on 90 of them.

**The verdict.** Against seeds 65-69 at generation 109 (the chain's spread): fastest 0.1259 against 0.1197-0.1298,
smallest 14,143 against 14,077-14,207, hypervolume 0.6002 - inside the replicates' spread; at its end (generation 155)
fastest 0.1204, smallest 14,127. **The four JIT genes (jitmul, jitdoes, jitexec, jitcfa) are on no design of the front**,
though the search tried them - 2,855 to 6,900 designs carried each, most of them alive: on the six selection workloads
they buy nothing and cost engine bytes, so every design with one is dominated by the same without it. What 471-490
measured is what the selection does not see - the applications' workload (the after-seed-70 batch) is the remedy.
appbench.py on 16 of the front: the fastest, 77d42fd7da (0.130), runs the seven applications at 0.756 of s6; Spearman
between the in-run speed and the applications' 0.553; every output right.

**The JIT at 32 bits, its first run**: the fastest at 32 bits 3d4e833b74, 0.130 of s6 (0.150 at 64); Spearman between
the two widths over the front 0.981. **906 designs failed at 32 bits, all with the JIT and tag 2** - the converter's JIT
map gave CELL+ and CELLS eight-byte cells at either width (found at 493, fixed in the after-seed-70 batch).

Facts: held out, sieve's Spearman with the selection 0.972 (fastest third 0.931, tenth 0.332), regret +1.5%; the fast
end's optimism 1.087 (the fastest tenth); remote checks dream 0.941 (default), 0.935 (32-bit), 0.959 (64-bit), the
Tegra 0.929 (8 designs); against the laptop's 32-bit speeds 0.893 (dream) and 0.800 (the Tegra); across the three
machine classes one design measured on all, ce7223f3f2 (0.402). The footprint experiment: the 16 fastest's order holds
better than before - Spearman 0.679 at 100 words, 0.465 at 10,000. Gene votes: jitos a candidate (12 of 12) - frozen in
the batch already; the four JIT genes four removal votes each - for want of the workload that pays for them.

---

# Evolved VM designs

31545 designs evaluated, 31545 alive, 8232 screened - not measured (Iteration 200). Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).


The Q-best design (binary + image x run time x memory - the knee; information): c5c95438a4, speed 0.146, binary + image 28,926, memory 160 KB - Q 0.206 of s6-cv8b's.

## The front's genomes

Each design above, and any other the front was measured again with, as one JSON line: its record without the raw timings. A tool that takes a database (--db) reads this report as one - the lines that are not JSON are skipped - so results/ is all a new chat needs to rebuild a front design.
